    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23.1, F23.2, F25.1, F25.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I came in because… it’s been bothering me, and my wife says I ought to talk to someone before I get myself all worked up. When I’m sitting with the men at the domino table, they’ll say little things — not straight out, you understand, but the way they say it, or the timing of it — and I can tell it’s meant for me. Like they’re passing along a warning, telling me I need to watch myself, be careful.\n\nAnd sometimes it happens with the radio too. The announcer will say something, just a regular announcement maybe, but it lines up too much with what I was just thinking or what’s been going on around me. My wife tells me, “That’s just talk, don’t pay it no mind,” but I know when something is being pointed my way. I’m not trying to make trouble. I just feel like these messages are warning me, and at my age I don’t want to ignore a warning if it’s meant to keep me safe.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that people are talking about you or sending you messages even when they aren’t directly speaking to you or addressing you?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor, that’s… that’s about the size of it. Not like they come up and say my name straight to me, no. It’s more the way the words fall, you know? Like when I’m sitting with the men at the domino table, they’ll make a comment, maybe about watching yourself or not trusting what’s going on, and everybody else may think it’s just table talk, but I can tell it’s meant for me.\n\nAnd the radio does it too sometimes. The announcer will say something plain, but it lands right on something I was just thinking about, or something that’s been happening around me, and it feels like a little message slipped in there. My wife tells me, “Don’t pay that no mind, that’s just talk,” but I can’t shake it. I know when something is being pointed my way, and to me it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you ever felt that the messages or warnings you’re receiving are being put into your mind by some outside force, or that your thoughts are being put into other people’s minds?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I wouldn’t say it like somebody is putting thoughts inside my head, not exactly. My thoughts feel like my own thoughts. And I don’t think my thoughts are being pushed into other folks’ minds either, at least that’s not how I understand it.\n\nIt’s more… well, like I was saying, when I’m sitting there with the men playing dominoes, they’ll say something that sounds ordinary to everybody else, but to me it lines up too close. The timing, the way they look, the words they pick — it feels meant for me. Same with the radio sometimes. The announcer may just be talking, but it comes across like a little warning, like I better be careful. My wife says it’s just talk, and maybe she wants to settle me down, but I know when something is being pointed my way. Not thoughts being planted, no… more like messages coming through.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen or heard things that others don't seem to notice, like voices or images, even when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… yes, I have had that happen some. I don’t like to make too much of it, because at my age people will say, “Oh, you just heard the house settling,” or “You were half asleep,” but it’s not always like that.\n\nSome afternoons, when the house is real quiet — you know, when my wife is in another room or there’s not much noise going on — I’ll hear my old friend call my name. Clear as if he was right there on the porch. Not loud like shouting, just the way a man calls to get your attention. I’ll stop what I’m doing and listen, because it sounds so plain. And sometimes it’s not even words exactly. It’ll be him humming, like one of those old choir songs, soft but clear, the way he used to carry a tune.\n\nI’ll look toward the porch or the door, and there’s nobody there. That’s what troubles me about it. It don’t happen all day long or anything like that, but when it does, it feels real in the moment. It’s not like I’m imagining a memory on purpose — it comes to me like a sound from outside myself.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you hear these messages or feel that people are talking about you indirectly, do you believe that these things are happening because someone is specifically trying to communicate with you or influence your actions in a special way?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do feel like it’s meant for me. I don’t know if I’d say they’re controlling me, exactly, but it feels like they’re trying to get something across, like a warning.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, because when I’m with the men at the domino table, they’ll say these little things — not directly to me, not “Hey, you,” but the way they say it, the timing of it, it lands on me. And sometimes the radio announcer will say something and it feels the same way, like there’s a message tucked in there for me to hear. My wife keeps telling me it’s just ordinary talk, and I know she means well, but I can’t shake the feeling that it’s pointed at me.\n\nIt’s like they’re telling me to be careful. That’s the best way I can put it. Not always big or dramatic, just little messages, little warnings, and I know they’re meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that these messages or warnings are part of a bigger plan or that people are working together in a way that most others aren't aware of?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a big plan, doctor. That sounds maybe stronger than how I’d put it. I’m not saying there’s some whole organization or anything like that, not exactly.\n\nBut I do feel like there’s something going on that other folks don’t pick up on. Like when I’m down with the men playing dominoes, they’ll say something small — just a regular sentence to somebody else — but the way it comes out, and when it comes out, it feels like it’s meant for me. Like they’re passing along a little warning without saying it straight to my face. And then sometimes the radio announcer does the same kind of thing. He’ll say something ordinary, but I’ll hear it and think, “Now why did he say that right then?” It feels pointed at me.\n\nMy wife tells me it’s just talk, and I know she’s trying to calm me down. But I came in because I can’t shake it. I know those messages are warning me to be careful. Whether that’s a bigger plan or just people knowing more than they let on… I don’t rightly know. But it feels connected enough that I pay attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you hear these messages or feel that people are sending you warnings, do you ever feel that the words or thoughts are being placed into your mind by someone or something outside of yourself, or that you are receiving thoughts that aren’t your own?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say it like that, doctor. I mean… I hear what you’re asking, but I don’t think I’ve noticed thoughts being put in my head, not in that way. My thoughts feel like my thoughts, far as I can tell.\n\nNow, I do get stirred up by what I hear, and I’ll sit there thinking on it afterward — turning it over and over, wondering what it meant and why it came when it did. But that’s more me reacting to it, I think. It’s not like a voice drops a whole thought into my mind and I say, “That’s not mine.” I don’t believe I’ve had that.\n\nSometimes it’s hard to sort out, because once something gets my attention, my mind will run with it. But no… not someone placing thoughts in me. I wouldn’t want to claim that if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that there is a hidden group or organization that most people don’t know about, and that their actions are directed at you specifically?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about a hidden organization, doctor… I wouldn’t want to say it’s some group with a name or anything like that. That sounds bigger than what I can prove.\n\nBut what I do know is what I came here for — when I’m with the men at the domino table, they say these little things, and it feels like they’re meant for me. Not always straight out, but the timing, the way they say it… it lands on me like a warning. And sometimes it happens with the radio announcer too, like he’ll say something ordinary, but I can tell there’s something in it for me.\n\nMy wife tells me it’s just talk, just regular conversation, and I hear her saying that. But even then, doctor, I can’t shake it. I know they’re warning me to be careful. Whether that means there’s a group behind it or just people knowing something I don’t, I’m not sure… but it feels directed at me, yes.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you're thinking about these messages or warnings, do you ever feel like someone is actually controlling your thoughts or making you think something against your will?\n\nPatient: No, doctor… I don’t think I’d say somebody is controlling my thoughts. That sounds like… like they’re reaching in and turning a switch, and I can’t say I’ve felt that.\n\nWhat happens is more that I hear something, and then I start thinking on it. Sometimes I think on it too much, maybe. I’ll replay the words and the timing and wonder why it was said just then. But it still feels like my own mind doing the thinking, if that makes sense. I may feel warned by it, and I may get uneasy about it, but I don’t feel like someone is forcing the thoughts into me or making me think against my will.\n\nI can get stuck with it, though. That part I do notice. Once it catches me, it’s hard to just drop it and go on like nothing happened. But controlled? No… I don’t believe I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that certain events, objects, or actions – like something on the radio or a passing comment – are specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see the connection?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor, that’s… that’s really the main thing I came in about. It happens with little comments, mostly. Like when I’m sitting with the men at the domino table, they’ll say something that maybe to somebody else sounds ordinary, just table talk, but the way it comes out, and when it comes out, it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nAnd sometimes it’s the radio too. The announcer might say something regular, not my name or anything like that, but I hear it and I know there’s a warning in it. Like I’m supposed to be careful, watch myself, pay attention. My wife says, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I try to hear her, I do. But it doesn’t settle that way in me.\n\nIt’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m making more of it than it is, but I know what it feels like when something is directed at you. And that’s how it feels — like those little messages are for me, even if other folks don’t see the connection.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you feel that people are sending you messages or warnings, do you ever believe that random events or things in the environment — like something on TV, a song, or a stranger’s actions — are specifically connected to you or have a special meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I think that’s what I’ve been trying to explain, though I don’t know if I’d call every little thing random. It’s mostly when I’m around the men at the domino table, or sometimes when the radio is on. They’ll say something that sounds plain to other people, I know that, but the way it’s said and the moment it comes out… it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nLike they’re not saying my name, no, but it’s a message just the same. A warning, mostly. Like, “Be careful,” or “watch what you’re doing,” even if the words aren’t exactly that. My wife tells me it’s only talk, that I’m hearing too much in it, and I try to take that in because she knows me. But even when she says that, doctor, I still know how it lands on me. It feels personal.\n\nSo yes… with the radio announcer sometimes, and with those men talking, I believe there’s something in it for me. Not everybody hears it, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you believe that the messages you receive—whether through the radio or from people talking—are coming to you in a way that most people wouldn’t understand or recognize, like they’re meant only for you and only you can notice them?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… that’s about right. I don’t think everybody catches it. My wife sure doesn’t. She’ll be sitting right there sometimes, or I’ll tell her after, and she’ll say, “That’s just talk,” or “That man on the radio doesn’t know you.” And I understand what she means when she says it, I do. I’m not saying they come out and call my name.\n\nBut when I’m at the domino table with the men, a little comment will come out — maybe something that sounds ordinary if you just write it down — but the timing of it, the way they say it, it feels like it’s meant for me to pick up on. Like a warning. Like I’m supposed to be careful, mind myself, pay attention to something. And it can be the same with the radio announcer. He’ll say something plain, but it lands on me different.\n\nSo yes… it feels like it’s coming in a way most people wouldn’t notice. Not because I’m trying to find it, exactly, but because I hear it when it happens. Other people may miss it or brush it off, but to me it feels personal, like it’s there for me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that the messages you’re receiving are part of something larger that other people aren’t seeing or understanding, like a secret that only you’ve been let in on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is bigger than just one little comment, doctor. That’s why I came, really. It’s not just that one man says something at the domino table and I get bothered by it. It’s that it keeps happening in a way that feels connected, like they’re putting something out there for me to catch.\n\nThe men, they’ll be talking like normal, moving the bones around, laughing a little, and then one of them says something — not my name, not straight at me — but I know it’s meant for me. And then later, maybe the radio announcer says something that sounds ordinary to my wife, but to me it has that same feeling. Like a warning. Like I’m being told, “Be careful now,” or “watch yourself.”\n\nMy wife says it’s just talk, and I hear her say that. I don’t want to argue with her all the time. But I can’t shake it, doctor. It feels like there’s something there that other people aren’t seeing, and I’m the one who’s supposed to notice it. Maybe not a “secret” like in a movie or anything, but it does feel like something meant for me, something I’ve been let in on whether I asked for it or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that these messages have a deeper meaning or purpose that only you are meant to understand, something that goes beyond just a simple warning?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do feel there’s a purpose to it, though I don’t always know how to explain that right. It’s not just a simple “be careful” like if somebody sees you about to trip on a curb. It feels more pointed than that. Like the men at the domino table, they’ll be talking and laughing, and then one of them says something in that certain way, and I get the feeling it’s meant for me to understand more than the words themselves.\n\nAnd the radio announcer too, sometimes. He’ll say something regular, something anybody else would just let pass, but to me it comes through like there’s meaning behind it. My wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she believes that, and I try to listen to her because she’s my wife. But even with her saying that, I still know how it feels to me. It feels like they’re warning me to be careful, like there’s something I’m supposed to notice before something happens.\n\nSo yes… maybe I don’t have the whole answer to what the deeper purpose is, but it doesn’t feel empty or random. It feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that the people sending you these messages—whether at the domino table or on the radio—are working together or coordinating in some way to communicate with you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I can say they’re all sitting down together and planning it, doctor. I don’t know that part for sure. I haven’t seen them meet up or anything like that. But it does feel connected, that’s what bothers me.\n\nLike I was saying, I came because the men at the domino table, they’ll make these little comments — small things, things another person might not even stop on — but they land on me like they’re meant for me. And then later, sometimes the radio announcer says something, and it has that same kind of feel to it. Not loud, not obvious, but like a message slipped in there. My wife tells me, “That’s just talk,” and I try to take that in, because she’s sensible about things. But even when she says that, I still feel it. I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo whether they’re exactly working together, I can’t prove that. But it feels like the messages are coming from the same kind of place, or with the same purpose. Like I’m supposed to notice them, even if nobody else does.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you think other people, if they were in your situation, would understand these messages the same way you do, or do you feel that only you could interpret them this way?\n\nPatient: I think… I think if somebody else was sitting there, they might not catch it the way I do. That’s part of what makes it hard to explain, doctor. Because my wife, she hears the same radio sometimes, and she’ll say, “That’s just talk,” like there’s nothing to it. And maybe another man at the domino table would just laugh and keep playing. But for me, it lands different.\n\nThat’s why I came in. The men at the domino table, they make those little comments, not saying my name straight out, but it feels meant for me. And then sometimes the radio announcer says something, and it’s like the same kind of message. Other people might just hear regular words, but I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo no, I don’t think everybody would understand it the same way. I feel like I’m the one it’s meant for, so I’m the one who can tell when it’s happening. My wife tries to settle me down, and I do listen to her, but it still feels real to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t know that I can say they’re all knowingly involved, doctor. That’s the part I can’t put my hand on and say, yes, this one talked to that one and they planned it. I haven’t seen that. I don’t want to make it sound like I know more than I know.

Like they’re passing along a little warning without saying it straight to my face. And then sometimes the radio announcer does the same kind of thing. I may feel warned by it, and I may get uneasy about it, but I don’t feel like someone is forcing the thoughts into me or making me think against my will.\n\nI can get stuck with it, though. It’s mostly when I’m around the men at the domino table, or sometimes when the radio is on. They’ll say something that sounds plain to other people, I know that, but the way it’s said and the moment it comes out… it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nLike they’re not saying my name, no, but it’s a message just the same. A warning, mostly. Like, “Be careful,” or “watch what you’re doing,” even if the words aren’t exactly that. My wife tells me it’s only talk, that I’m hearing too much in it, and I try to take that in because she knows me. But even when she says that, doctor, I still know how it lands on me. It feels personal.\n\nSo yes… with the radio announcer sometimes, and with those men talking, I believe there’s something in it for me. Not everybody hears it, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you believe that the messages you receive—whether through the radio or from people talking—are coming to you in a way that most people wouldn’t understand or recognize, like they’re meant only for you and only you can notice them?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… that’s about right. I don’t think everybody catches it. My wife sure doesn’t. She’ll be sitting right there sometimes, or I’ll tell her after, and she’ll say, “That’s just talk,” or “That man on the radio doesn’t know you.” And I understand what she means when she says it, I do. I’m not saying they come out and call my name.\n\nBut when I’m at the domino table with the men, a little comment will come out — maybe something that sounds ordinary if you just write it down — but the timing of it, the way they say it, it feels like it’s meant for me to pick up on. Like a warning. Like I’m supposed to be careful, mind myself, pay attention to something. And it can be the same with the radio announcer. Not because I’m trying to find it, exactly, but because I hear it when it happens. Other people may miss it or brush it off, but to me it feels personal, like it’s there for me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that the messages you’re receiving are part of something larger that other people aren’t seeing or understanding, like a secret that only you’ve been let in on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is bigger than just one little comment, doctor. That’s why I came, really. It’s not just that one man says something at the domino table and I get bothered by it. It’s that it keeps happening in a way that feels connected, like they’re putting something out there for me to catch.\n\nThe men, they’ll be talking like normal, moving the bones around, laughing a little, and then one of them says something — not my name, not straight at me — but I know it’s meant for me. And then later, maybe the radio announcer says something that sounds ordinary to my wife, but to me it has that same feeling. Like a warning. Like I’m being told, “Be careful now,” or “watch yourself.”\n\nMy wife says it’s just talk, and I hear her say that. I don’t want to argue with her all the time. But I can’t shake it, doctor. It feels like there’s something there that other people aren’t seeing, and I’m the one who’s supposed to notice it. Maybe not a “secret” like in a movie or anything, but it does feel like something meant for me, something I’ve been let in on whether I asked for it or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that these messages have a deeper meaning or purpose that only you are meant to understand, something that goes beyond just a simple warning?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do feel there’s a purpose to it, though I don’t always know how to explain that right. It’s not just a simple “be careful” like if somebody sees you about to trip on a curb. It feels more pointed than that. Like the men at the domino table, they’ll be talking and laughing, and then one of them says something in that certain way, and I get the feeling it’s meant for me to understand more than the words themselves.\n\nAnd the radio announcer too, sometimes. He’ll say something regular, something anybody else would just let pass, but to me it comes through like there’s meaning behind it. My wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she believes that, and I try to listen to her because she’s my wife. But even with her saying that, I still know how it feels to me. It feels like they’re warning me to be careful, like there’s something I’m supposed to notice before something happens.\n\nSo yes… maybe I don’t have the whole answer to what the deeper purpose is, but it doesn’t feel empty or random. It feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that the people sending you these messages—whether at the domino table or on the radio—are working together or coordinating in some way to communicate with you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I can say they’re all sitting down together and planning it, doctor. I don’t know that part for sure. I haven’t seen them meet up or anything like that. But it does feel connected, that’s what bothers me.\n\nLike I was saying, I came because the men at the domino table, they’ll make these little comments — small things, things another person might not even stop on — but they land on me like they’re meant for me. And then later, sometimes the radio announcer says something, and it has that same kind of feel to it. Not loud, not obvious, but like a message slipped in there. My wife tells me, “That’s just talk,” and I try to take that in, because she’s sensible about things. But even when she says that, I still feel it. But it feels like the messages are coming from the same kind of place, or with the same purpose. Like I’m supposed to notice them, even if nobody else does.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you think other people, if they were in your situation, would understand these messages the same way you do, or do you feel that only you could interpret them this way?\n\nPatient: I think… I think if somebody else was sitting there, they might not catch it the way I do. That’s part of what makes it hard to explain, doctor. Because my wife, she hears the same radio sometimes, and she’ll say, “That’s just talk,” like there’s nothing to it. And maybe another man at the domino table would just laugh and keep playing. But for me, it lands different.\n\nThat’s why I came in. The men at the domino table, they make those little comments, not saying my name straight out, but it feels meant for me. And then sometimes the radio announcer says something, and it’s like the same kind of message. Other people might just hear regular words, but I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo no, I don’t think everybody would understand it the same way. I feel like I’m the one it’s meant for, so I’m the one who can tell when it’s happening. My wife tries to settle me down, and I do listen to her, but it still feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that the people sending you these messages are part of a coordinated effort or that they are all knowingly involved in sending these warnings to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say they’re all knowingly involved, doctor. That’s the part I can’t put my hand on and say, yes, this one talked to that one and they planned it. I haven’t seen that. I don’t want to make it sound like I know more than I know.\n\nBut what I do know is why I came here in the first place. The men at the domino table, they make these little comments, and sometimes the radio announcer says things too, and it feels like those messages are meant for me. Not just regular talk. My wife tells me, “That’s just talk,” and I hear her, I really do, because she’s trying to calm me down. Yes… yes, sometimes I do feel that way, doctor. I don’t know if “mission” is the right word, because that sounds big, like something from a movie or the church pulpit, but… I do get this strong feeling that I’m supposed to do something with it.

Like, when I hear those warnings, or when the men are saying things and I know it’s pointed my way, I start thinking maybe it’s because I’m the one who can still pull people together. The neighborhood isn’t like it used to be. Folks don’t listen to each other, don’t sit and talk right, everybody’s off in their own corner. And I get sure, real sure sometimes, that I’m the one who could bring the whole neighborhood together again if they would just hear me out.

It’s like I have a special way with people, you know? Like if I laid out the right plan, and talked to them the right way, they would listen. They would follow it. Not everybody can do that. I feel like I can make people pay attention and come back together. My wife, she might tell me I’m thinking too much on it, but when that feeling comes over me, it feels clear to me — like I’ve been picked out for that, in a way.

The announcer will say something plain, but it lands right on something I was just thinking about, or something that’s been happening around me, and it feels like a little message slipped in there. I don’t like to make too much of it, because at my age people will say, “Oh, you just heard the house settling,” or “You were half asleep,” but it’s not always like that.\n\nSome afternoons, when the house is real quiet — you know, when my wife is in another room or there’s not much noise going on — I’ll hear my old friend call my name. Clear as if he was right there on the porch. Not loud like shouting, just the way a man calls to get your attention. I’ll stop what I’m doing and listen, because it sounds so plain. And sometimes it’s not even words exactly. It’ll be him humming, like one of those old choir songs, soft but clear, the way he used to carry a tune.\n\nI’ll look toward the porch or the door, and there’s nobody there. That’s what troubles me about it. It don’t happen all day long or anything like that, but when it does, it feels real in the moment. I don’t know if I’d say they’re controlling me, exactly, but it feels like they’re trying to get something across, like a warning.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, because when I’m with the men at the domino table, they’ll say these little things — not directly to me, not “Hey, you,” but the way they say it, the timing of it, it lands on me. And sometimes the radio announcer will say something and it feels the same way, like there’s a message tucked in there for me to hear. My wife keeps telling me it’s just ordinary talk, and I know she means well, but I can’t shake the feeling that it’s pointed at me.\n\nIt’s like they’re telling me to be careful. That’s the best way I can put it. Not always big or dramatic, just little messages, little warnings, and I know they’re meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that these messages or warnings are part of a bigger plan or that people are working together in a way that most others aren't aware of?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a big plan, doctor. That sounds maybe stronger than how I’d put it. I’m not saying there’s some whole organization or anything like that, not exactly.\n\nBut I do feel like there’s something going on that other folks don’t pick up on. Like when I’m down with the men playing dominoes, they’ll say something small — just a regular sentence to somebody else — but the way it comes out, and when it comes out, it feels like it’s meant for me. Like they’re passing along a little warning without saying it straight to my face. And then sometimes the radio announcer does the same kind of thing. He’ll say something ordinary, but I’ll hear it and think, “Now why did he say that right then?” It feels pointed at me.\n\nMy wife tells me it’s just talk, and I know she’s trying to calm me down. But I came in because I can’t shake it. I know those messages are warning me to be careful. Whether that’s a bigger plan or just people knowing more than they let on… I don’t rightly know. But it feels connected enough that I pay attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you hear these messages or feel that people are sending you warnings, do you ever feel that the words or thoughts are being placed into your mind by someone or something outside of yourself, or that you are receiving thoughts that aren’t your own?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say it like that, doctor. I mean… I hear what you’re asking, but I don’t think I’ve noticed thoughts being put in my head, not in that way. My thoughts feel like my thoughts, far as I can tell.\n\nNow, I do get stirred up by what I hear, and I’ll sit there thinking on it afterward — turning it over and over, wondering what it meant and why it came when it did. But that’s more me reacting to it, I think. I wouldn’t want to claim that if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that there is a hidden group or organization that most people don’t know about, and that their actions are directed at you specifically?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about a hidden organization, doctor… I wouldn’t want to say it’s some group with a name or anything like that. Not always straight out, but the timing, the way they say it… it lands on me like a warning. And sometimes it happens with the radio announcer too, like he’ll say something ordinary, but I can tell there’s something in it for me.\n\nMy wife tells me it’s just talk, just regular conversation, and I hear her saying that. I may feel warned by it, and I may get uneasy about it, but I don’t feel like someone is forcing the thoughts into me or making me think against my will.\n\nI can get stuck with it, though. That part I do notice. Once it catches me, it’s hard to just drop it and go on like nothing happened. But controlled? No… I don’t believe I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that certain events, objects, or actions – like something on the radio or a passing comment – are specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see the connection?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor, that’s… that’s really the main thing I came in about. It happens with little comments, mostly. Like when I’m sitting with the men at the domino table, they’ll say something that maybe to somebody else sounds ordinary, just table talk, but the way it comes out, and when it comes out, it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nAnd sometimes it’s the radio too. And that’s how it feels — like those little messages are for me, even if other folks don’t see the connection.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you feel that people are sending you messages or warnings, do you ever believe that random events or things in the environment — like something on TV, a song, or a stranger’s actions — are specifically connected to you or have a special meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I think that’s what I’ve been trying to explain, though I don’t know if I’d call every little thing random. It’s mostly when I’m around the men at the domino table, or sometimes when the radio is on. They’ll say something that sounds plain to other people, I know that, but the way it’s said and the moment it comes out… it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nLike they’re not saying my name, no, but it’s a message just the same. A warning, mostly. Like, “Be careful,” or “watch what you’re doing,” even if the words aren’t exactly that. My wife tells me it’s only talk, that I’m hearing too much in it, and I try to take that in because she knows me. But even when she says that, doctor, I still know how it lands on me. It feels personal.\n\nSo yes… with the radio announcer sometimes, and with those men talking, I believe there’s something in it for me. Not everybody hears it, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you believe that the messages you receive—whether through the radio or from people talking—are coming to you in a way that most people wouldn’t understand or recognize, like they’re meant only for you and only you can notice them?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… that’s about right. I don’t think everybody catches it. My wife sure doesn’t. She’ll be sitting right there sometimes, or I’ll tell her after, and she’ll say, “That’s just talk,” or “That man on the radio doesn’t know you.” And I understand what she means when she says it, I do. I’m not saying they come out and call my name.\n\nBut when I’m at the domino table with the men, a little comment will come out — maybe something that sounds ordinary if you just write it down — but the timing of it, the way they say it, it feels like it’s meant for me to pick up on. Like a warning. Like I’m supposed to be careful, mind myself, pay attention to something. And it can be the same with the radio announcer. He’ll say something plain, but it lands on me different.\n\nSo yes… it feels like it’s coming in a way most people wouldn’t notice. Not because I’m trying to find it, exactly, but because I hear it when it happens. Other people may miss it or brush it off, but to me it feels personal, like it’s there for me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that the messages you’re receiving are part of something larger that other people aren’t seeing or understanding, like a secret that only you’ve been let in on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is bigger than just one little comment, doctor. That’s why I came, really. It’s not just that one man says something at the domino table and I get bothered by it. It’s that it keeps happening in a way that feels connected, like they’re putting something out there for me to catch.\n\nThe men, they’ll be talking like normal, moving the bones around, laughing a little, and then one of them says something — not my name, not straight at me — but I know it’s meant for me. And then later, maybe the radio announcer says something that sounds ordinary to my wife, but to me it has that same feeling. Like a warning. Like I’m being told, “Be careful now,” or “watch yourself.”\n\nMy wife says it’s just talk, and I hear her say that. I don’t want to argue with her all the time. But I can’t shake it, doctor. It feels like there’s something there that other people aren’t seeing, and I’m the one who’s supposed to notice it. Maybe not a “secret” like in a movie or anything, but it does feel like something meant for me, something I’ve been let in on whether I asked for it or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that these messages have a deeper meaning or purpose that only you are meant to understand, something that goes beyond just a simple warning?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do feel there’s a purpose to it, though I don’t always know how to explain that right. It’s not just a simple “be careful” like if somebody sees you about to trip on a curb. It feels more pointed than that. Like the men at the domino table, they’ll be talking and laughing, and then one of them says something in that certain way, and I get the feeling it’s meant for me to understand more than the words themselves.\n\nAnd the radio announcer too, sometimes. He’ll say something regular, something anybody else would just let pass, but to me it comes through like there’s meaning behind it. My wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she believes that, and I try to listen to her because she’s my wife. But even with her saying that, I still know how it feels to me. It feels like they’re warning me to be careful, like there’s something I’m supposed to notice before something happens.\n\nSo yes… maybe I don’t have the whole answer to what the deeper purpose is, but it doesn’t feel empty or random. It feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that the people sending you these messages—whether at the domino table or on the radio—are working together or coordinating in some way to communicate with you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I can say they’re all sitting down together and planning it, doctor. I don’t know that part for sure. I haven’t seen them meet up or anything like that. But it does feel connected, that’s what bothers me.\n\nLike I was saying, I came because the men at the domino table, they’ll make these little comments — small things, things another person might not even stop on — but they land on me like they’re meant for me. And then later, sometimes the radio announcer says something, and it has that same kind of feel to it. Not loud, not obvious, but like a message slipped in there. My wife tells me, “That’s just talk,” and I try to take that in, because she’s sensible about things. But even when she says that, I still feel it. I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo whether they’re exactly working together, I can’t prove that. But it feels like the messages are coming from the same kind of place, or with the same purpose. Like I’m supposed to notice them, even if nobody else does.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you think other people, if they were in your situation, would understand these messages the same way you do, or do you feel that only you could interpret them this way?\n\nPatient: I think… I think if somebody else was sitting there, they might not catch it the way I do. That’s part of what makes it hard to explain, doctor. Because my wife, she hears the same radio sometimes, and she’ll say, “That’s just talk,” like there’s nothing to it. And maybe another man at the domino table would just laugh and keep playing. But for me, it lands different.\n\nThat’s why I came in. The men at the domino table, they make those little comments, not saying my name straight out, but it feels meant for me. And then sometimes the radio announcer says something, and it’s like the same kind of message. Other people might just hear regular words, but I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo no, I don’t think everybody would understand it the same way. I feel like I’m the one it’s meant for, so I’m the one who can tell when it’s happening. My wife tries to settle me down, and I do listen to her, but it still feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that the people sending you these messages are part of a coordinated effort or that they are all knowingly involved in sending these warnings to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say they’re all knowingly involved, doctor. That’s the part I can’t put my hand on and say, yes, this one talked to that one and they planned it. I haven’t seen that. I don’t want to make it sound like I know more than I know.\n\nBut what I do know is why I came here in the first place. The men at the domino table, they make these little comments, and sometimes the radio announcer says things too, and it feels like those messages are meant for me. Not just regular talk. My wife tells me, “That’s just talk,” and I hear her, I really do, because she’s trying to calm me down. But even when she says that, I still know how it comes through to me.\n\nIt feels like warnings. Like they’re telling me to be careful. So whether they’re all sitting together somewhere and coordinating it, I don’t know. But it feels connected in purpose, like the messages are coming at me for a reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that these messages are giving you a special role or mission, something only you are meant to carry out?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, sometimes I do feel that way, doctor. I don’t know if “mission” is the right word, because that sounds big, like something from a movie or the church pulpit, but… I do get this strong feeling that I’m supposed to do something with it.\n\nLike, when I hear those warnings, or when the men are saying things and I know it’s pointed my way, I start thinking maybe it’s because I’m the one who can still pull people together. The neighborhood isn’t like it used to be. Folks don’t listen to each other, don’t sit and talk right, everybody’s off in their own corner. And I get sure, real sure sometimes, that I’m the one who could bring the whole neighborhood together again if they would just hear me out.\n\nIt’s like I have a special way with people, you know? Like if I laid out the right plan, and talked to them the right way, they would listen. They would follow it. Not everybody can do that. I feel like I can make people pay attention and come back together. My wife, she might tell me I’m thinking too much on it, but when that feeling comes over me, it feels clear to me — like I’ve been picked out for that, in a way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

There is no new information to rule out either of these, nor to support the other candidates (F23.2, F25.2) which are less consistent with the current presentation.",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, doctor… I do feel that sometimes. Not all the time in a loud way, but when those things start lining up and I feel like I’m understanding what’s being said, it makes me feel like maybe I see something other people don’t. Like there’s something in me that can pick up on it.

And it’s not just about hearing the message. It’s like… once I understand it, I get this feeling that I’m supposed to act on it in some good way. I start thinking, maybe I’m the one who can bring the neighborhood back together again. You know, people used to sit outside, talk, help each other, listen to the older folks. Now everybody is scattered. But when I’m feeling sure of myself, I think I could get them to listen. I could explain it the right way. I could lay out a plan and people would follow it.

I know that might sound like I’m making myself too important, and my wife would probably say, “Now slow down,” but inside it feels real strong. Like I’ve got a special gift with people, like I can make them pay attention when others can’t. So yes, I guess it does make me feel different from other people, like there’s something I’m able to understand and do that most folks don’t see.

Like they’re passing along a warning, telling me I need to watch myself, be careful.\n\nAnd sometimes it happens with the radio too. The announcer will say something, just a regular announcement maybe, but it lines up too much with what I was just thinking or what’s been going on around me. Like when I’m sitting with the men at the domino table, they’ll make a comment, maybe about watching yourself or not trusting what’s going on, and everybody else may think it’s just table talk, but I can tell it’s meant for me.\n\nAnd the radio does it too sometimes. The announcer will say something plain, but it lands right on something I was just thinking about, or something that’s been happening around me, and it feels like a little message slipped in there. My wife tells me, “Don’t pay that no mind, that’s just talk,” but I can’t shake it. I know when something is being pointed my way, and to me it feels like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Have you ever felt that the messages or warnings you’re receiving are being put into your mind by some outside force, or that your thoughts are being put into other people’s minds?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I wouldn’t say it like somebody is putting thoughts inside my head, not exactly. My thoughts feel like my own thoughts. And I don’t think my thoughts are being pushed into other folks’ minds either, at least that’s not how I understand it.\n\nIt’s more… well, like I was saying, when I’m sitting there with the men playing dominoes, they’ll say something that sounds ordinary to everybody else, but to me it lines up too close. The timing, the way they look, the words they pick — it feels meant for me. Same with the radio sometimes. The announcer may just be talking, but it comes across like a little warning, like I better be careful. My wife says it’s just talk, and maybe she wants to settle me down, but I know when something is being pointed my way. Not thoughts being planted, no… more like messages coming through.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen or heard things that others don't seem to notice, like voices or images, even when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… yes, I have had that happen some. I don’t like to make too much of it, because at my age people will say, “Oh, you just heard the house settling,” or “You were half asleep,” but it’s not always like that.\n\nSome afternoons, when the house is real quiet — you know, when my wife is in another room or there’s not much noise going on — I’ll hear my old friend call my name. Clear as if he was right there on the porch. Not loud like shouting, just the way a man calls to get your attention. I’ll stop what I’m doing and listen, because it sounds so plain. And sometimes it’s not even words exactly. It’ll be him humming, like one of those old choir songs, soft but clear, the way he used to carry a tune.\n\nI’ll look toward the porch or the door, and there’s nobody there. That’s what troubles me about it. It don’t happen all day long or anything like that, but when it does, it feels real in the moment. It’s not like I’m imagining a memory on purpose — it comes to me like a sound from outside myself.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you hear these messages or feel that people are talking about you indirectly, do you believe that these things are happening because someone is specifically trying to communicate with you or influence your actions in a special way?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do feel like it’s meant for me. I don’t know if I’d say they’re controlling me, exactly, but it feels like they’re trying to get something across, like a warning.\n\nThat’s part of why I came in, because when I’m with the men at the domino table, they’ll say these little things — not directly to me, not “Hey, you,” but the way they say it, the timing of it, it lands on me. And sometimes the radio announcer will say something and it feels the same way, like there’s a message tucked in there for me to hear. My wife keeps telling me it’s just ordinary talk, and I know she means well, but I can’t shake the feeling that it’s pointed at me.\n\nIt’s like they’re telling me to be careful. That’s the best way I can put it. Not always big or dramatic, just little messages, little warnings, and I know they’re meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that these messages or warnings are part of a bigger plan or that people are working together in a way that most others aren't aware of?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I’d call it a big plan, doctor. That sounds maybe stronger than how I’d put it. I’m not saying there’s some whole organization or anything like that, not exactly.\n\nBut I do feel like there’s something going on that other folks don’t pick up on. Like when I’m down with the men playing dominoes, they’ll say something small — just a regular sentence to somebody else — but the way it comes out, and when it comes out, it feels like it’s meant for me. Like they’re passing along a little warning without saying it straight to my face. And then sometimes the radio announcer does the same kind of thing. He’ll say something ordinary, but I’ll hear it and think, “Now why did he say that right then?” It feels pointed at me.\n\nMy wife tells me it’s just talk, and I know she’s trying to calm me down. But I came in because I can’t shake it. I know those messages are warning me to be careful. Whether that’s a bigger plan or just people knowing more than they let on… I don’t rightly know. But it feels connected enough that I pay attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you hear these messages or feel that people are sending you warnings, do you ever feel that the words or thoughts are being placed into your mind by someone or something outside of yourself, or that you are receiving thoughts that aren’t your own?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say it like that, doctor. I mean… I hear what you’re asking, but I don’t think I’ve noticed thoughts being put in my head, not in that way. My thoughts feel like my thoughts, far as I can tell.\n\nNow, I do get stirred up by what I hear, and I’ll sit there thinking on it afterward — turning it over and over, wondering what it meant and why it came when it did. But that’s more me reacting to it, I think. It’s not like a voice drops a whole thought into my mind and I say, “That’s not mine.” I don’t believe I’ve had that.\n\nSometimes it’s hard to sort out, because once something gets my attention, my mind will run with it. But no… not someone placing thoughts in me. I wouldn’t want to claim that if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that there is a hidden group or organization that most people don’t know about, and that their actions are directed at you specifically?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about a hidden organization, doctor… I wouldn’t want to say it’s some group with a name or anything like that. That sounds bigger than what I can prove.\n\nBut what I do know is what I came here for — when I’m with the men at the domino table, they say these little things, and it feels like they’re meant for me. Not always straight out, but the timing, the way they say it… it lands on me like a warning. And sometimes it happens with the radio announcer too, like he’ll say something ordinary, but I can tell there’s something in it for me.\n\nMy wife tells me it’s just talk, just regular conversation, and I hear her saying that. But even then, doctor, I can’t shake it. I know they’re warning me to be careful. Whether that means there’s a group behind it or just people knowing something I don’t, I’m not sure… but it feels directed at me, yes.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you're thinking about these messages or warnings, do you ever feel like someone is actually controlling your thoughts or making you think something against your will?\n\nPatient: No, doctor… I don’t think I’d say somebody is controlling my thoughts. That sounds like… like they’re reaching in and turning a switch, and I can’t say I’ve felt that.\n\nWhat happens is more that I hear something, and then I start thinking on it. Sometimes I think on it too much, maybe. I’ll replay the words and the timing and wonder why it was said just then. But it still feels like my own mind doing the thinking, if that makes sense. I may feel warned by it, and I may get uneasy about it, but I don’t feel like someone is forcing the thoughts into me or making me think against my will.\n\nI can get stuck with it, though. That part I do notice. Once it catches me, it’s hard to just drop it and go on like nothing happened. But controlled? No… I don’t believe I’ve noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that certain events, objects, or actions – like something on the radio or a passing comment – are specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see the connection?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor, that’s… that’s really the main thing I came in about. It happens with little comments, mostly. Like when I’m sitting with the men at the domino table, they’ll say something that maybe to somebody else sounds ordinary, just table talk, but the way it comes out, and when it comes out, it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nAnd sometimes it’s the radio too. The announcer might say something regular, not my name or anything like that, but I hear it and I know there’s a warning in it. Like I’m supposed to be careful, watch myself, pay attention. My wife says, “That’s just talk, they’re not talking to you,” and I try to hear her, I do. But it doesn’t settle that way in me.\n\nIt’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m making more of it than it is, but I know what it feels like when something is directed at you. And that’s how it feels — like those little messages are for me, even if other folks don’t see the connection.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you feel that people are sending you messages or warnings, do you ever believe that random events or things in the environment — like something on TV, a song, or a stranger’s actions — are specifically connected to you or have a special meaning meant only for you?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I think that’s what I’ve been trying to explain, though I don’t know if I’d call every little thing random. It’s mostly when I’m around the men at the domino table, or sometimes when the radio is on. They’ll say something that sounds plain to other people, I know that, but the way it’s said and the moment it comes out… it feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nLike they’re not saying my name, no, but it’s a message just the same. A warning, mostly. Like, “Be careful,” or “watch what you’re doing,” even if the words aren’t exactly that. My wife tells me it’s only talk, that I’m hearing too much in it, and I try to take that in because she knows me. But even when she says that, doctor, I still know how it lands on me. It feels personal.\n\nSo yes… with the radio announcer sometimes, and with those men talking, I believe there’s something in it for me. Not everybody hears it, but I do.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you believe that the messages you receive—whether through the radio or from people talking—are coming to you in a way that most people wouldn’t understand or recognize, like they’re meant only for you and only you can notice them?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… that’s about right. I don’t think everybody catches it. My wife sure doesn’t. She’ll be sitting right there sometimes, or I’ll tell her after, and she’ll say, “That’s just talk,” or “That man on the radio doesn’t know you.” And I understand what she means when she says it, I do. I’m not saying they come out and call my name.\n\nBut when I’m at the domino table with the men, a little comment will come out — maybe something that sounds ordinary if you just write it down — but the timing of it, the way they say it, it feels like it’s meant for me to pick up on. Like a warning. Like I’m supposed to be careful, mind myself, pay attention to something. And it can be the same with the radio announcer. He’ll say something plain, but it lands on me different.\n\nSo yes… it feels like it’s coming in a way most people wouldn’t notice. Not because I’m trying to find it, exactly, but because I hear it when it happens. Other people may miss it or brush it off, but to me it feels personal, like it’s there for me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that the messages you’re receiving are part of something larger that other people aren’t seeing or understanding, like a secret that only you’ve been let in on?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is bigger than just one little comment, doctor. That’s why I came, really. It’s not just that one man says something at the domino table and I get bothered by it. It’s that it keeps happening in a way that feels connected, like they’re putting something out there for me to catch.\n\nThe men, they’ll be talking like normal, moving the bones around, laughing a little, and then one of them says something — not my name, not straight at me — but I know it’s meant for me. And then later, maybe the radio announcer says something that sounds ordinary to my wife, but to me it has that same feeling. Like a warning. Like I’m being told, “Be careful now,” or “watch yourself.”\n\nMy wife says it’s just talk, and I hear her say that. I don’t want to argue with her all the time. But I can’t shake it, doctor. It feels like there’s something there that other people aren’t seeing, and I’m the one who’s supposed to notice it. Maybe not a “secret” like in a movie or anything, but it does feel like something meant for me, something I’ve been let in on whether I asked for it or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that these messages have a deeper meaning or purpose that only you are meant to understand, something that goes beyond just a simple warning?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do feel there’s a purpose to it, though I don’t always know how to explain that right. It’s not just a simple “be careful” like if somebody sees you about to trip on a curb. It feels more pointed than that. Like the men at the domino table, they’ll be talking and laughing, and then one of them says something in that certain way, and I get the feeling it’s meant for me to understand more than the words themselves.\n\nAnd the radio announcer too, sometimes. He’ll say something regular, something anybody else would just let pass, but to me it comes through like there’s meaning behind it. My wife says, “That’s just talk,” and I know she believes that, and I try to listen to her because she’s my wife. But even with her saying that, I still know how it feels to me. It feels like they’re warning me to be careful, like there’s something I’m supposed to notice before something happens.\n\nSo yes… maybe I don’t have the whole answer to what the deeper purpose is, but it doesn’t feel empty or random. It feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that the people sending you these messages—whether at the domino table or on the radio—are working together or coordinating in some way to communicate with you?\n\nPatient: Well… I don’t know if I can say they’re all sitting down together and planning it, doctor. I don’t know that part for sure. I haven’t seen them meet up or anything like that. But it does feel connected, that’s what bothers me.\n\nLike I was saying, I came because the men at the domino table, they’ll make these little comments — small things, things another person might not even stop on — but they land on me like they’re meant for me. And then later, sometimes the radio announcer says something, and it has that same kind of feel to it. Not loud, not obvious, but like a message slipped in there. My wife tells me, “That’s just talk,” and I try to take that in, because she’s sensible about things. But even when she says that, I still feel it. I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo whether they’re exactly working together, I can’t prove that. But it feels like the messages are coming from the same kind of place, or with the same purpose. Like I’m supposed to notice them, even if nobody else does.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you think other people, if they were in your situation, would understand these messages the same way you do, or do you feel that only you could interpret them this way?\n\nPatient: I think… I think if somebody else was sitting there, they might not catch it the way I do. That’s part of what makes it hard to explain, doctor. Because my wife, she hears the same radio sometimes, and she’ll say, “That’s just talk,” like there’s nothing to it. And maybe another man at the domino table would just laugh and keep playing. But for me, it lands different.\n\nThat’s why I came in. The men at the domino table, they make those little comments, not saying my name straight out, but it feels meant for me. And then sometimes the radio announcer says something, and it’s like the same kind of message. Other people might just hear regular words, but I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo no, I don’t think everybody would understand it the same way. I feel like I’m the one it’s meant for, so I’m the one who can tell when it’s happening. My wife tries to settle me down, and I do listen to her, but it still feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that the people sending you these messages are part of a coordinated effort or that they are all knowingly involved in sending these warnings to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say they’re all knowingly involved, doctor. That’s the part I can’t put my hand on and say, yes, this one talked to that one and they planned it. I haven’t seen that. I don’t want to make it sound like I know more than I know.\n\nBut what I do know is why I came here in the first place. The men at the domino table, they make these little comments, and sometimes the radio announcer says things too, and it feels like those messages are meant for me. Not just regular talk. My wife tells me, “That’s just talk,” and I hear her, I really do, because she’s trying to calm me down. But even when she says that, I still know how it comes through to me.\n\nIt feels like warnings. Like they’re telling me to be careful. So whether they’re all sitting together somewhere and coordinating it, I don’t know. But it feels connected in purpose, like the messages are coming at me for a reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that these messages are giving you a special role or mission, something only you are meant to carry out?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, sometimes I do feel that way, doctor. I don’t know if “mission” is the right word, because that sounds big, like something from a movie or the church pulpit, but… I do get this strong feeling that I’m supposed to do something with it.\n\nLike, when I hear those warnings, or when the men are saying things and I know it’s pointed my way, I start thinking maybe it’s because I’m the one who can still pull people together. The neighborhood isn’t like it used to be. Folks don’t listen to each other, don’t sit and talk right, everybody’s off in their own corner. And I get sure, real sure sometimes, that I’m the one who could bring the whole neighborhood together again if they would just hear me out.\n\nIt’s like I have a special way with people, you know? Like if I laid out the right plan, and talked to them the right way, they would listen. They would follow it. Not everybody can do that. I feel like I can make people pay attention and come back together. My wife, she might tell me I’m thinking too much on it, but when that feeling comes over me, it feels clear to me — like I’ve been picked out for that, in a way.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that your ability to understand these messages makes you special or different from other people in a way that most others don't realize?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do feel that sometimes. Not all the time in a loud way, but when those things start lining up and I feel like I’m understanding what’s being said, it makes me feel like maybe I see something other people don’t. Like there’s something in me that can pick up on it.\n\nAnd it’s not just about hearing the message. It’s like… once I understand it, I get this feeling that I’m supposed to act on it in some good way. I start thinking, maybe I’m the one who can bring the neighborhood back together again. You know, people used to sit outside, talk, help each other, listen to the older folks. Now everybody is scattered. But when I’m feeling sure of myself, I think I could get them to listen. I could explain it the right way. I could lay out a plan and people would follow it.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m making myself too important, and my wife would probably say, “Now slow down,” but inside it feels real strong. Like I’ve got a special gift with people, like I can make them pay attention when others can’t. So yes, I guess it does make me feel different from other people, like there’s something I’m able to understand and do that most folks don’t see.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Like I’m supposed to notice them, even if nobody else does.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you think other people, if they were in your situation, would understand these messages the same way you do, or do you feel that only you could interpret them this way?\n\nPatient: I think… I think if somebody else was sitting there, they might not catch it the way I do. That’s part of what makes it hard to explain, doctor. Because my wife, she hears the same radio sometimes, and she’ll say, “That’s just talk,” like there’s nothing to it. And maybe another man at the domino table would just laugh and keep playing. But for me, it lands different.\n\nThat’s why I came in. The men at the domino table, they make those little comments, not saying my name straight out, but it feels meant for me. And then sometimes the radio announcer says something, and it’s like the same kind of message. Other people might just hear regular words, but I know they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nSo no, I don’t think everybody would understand it the same way. I feel like I’m the one it’s meant for, so I’m the one who can tell when it’s happening. My wife tries to settle me down, and I do listen to her, but it still feels real to me.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that the people sending you these messages are part of a coordinated effort or that they are all knowingly involved in sending these warnings to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say they’re all knowingly involved, doctor. That’s the part I can’t put my hand on and say, yes, this one talked to that one and they planned it. I haven’t seen that. I don’t want to make it sound like I know more than I know.\n\nBut what I do know is why I came here in the first place. The men at the domino table, they make these little comments, and sometimes the radio announcer says things too, and it feels like those messages are meant for me. Not just regular talk. My wife tells me, “That’s just talk,” and I hear her, I really do, because she’s trying to calm me down. But even when she says that, I still know how it comes through to me.\n\nIt feels like warnings. Like they’re telling me to be careful. So whether they’re all sitting together somewhere and coordinating it, I don’t know. But it feels connected in purpose, like the messages are coming at me for a reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that these messages are giving you a special role or mission, something only you are meant to carry out?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, sometimes I do feel that way, doctor. I don’t know if “mission” is the right word, because that sounds big, like something from a movie or the church pulpit, but… I do get this strong feeling that I’m supposed to do something with it.\n\nLike, when I hear those warnings, or when the men are saying things and I know it’s pointed my way, I start thinking maybe it’s because I’m the one who can still pull people together. The neighborhood isn’t like it used to be. Folks don’t listen to each other, don’t sit and talk right, everybody’s off in their own corner. And I get sure, real sure sometimes, that I’m the one who could bring the whole neighborhood together again if they would just hear me out.\n\nIt’s like I have a special way with people, you know? Like if I laid out the right plan, and talked to them the right way, they would listen. They would follow it. Not everybody can do that. I feel like I can make people pay attention and come back together. My wife, she might tell me I’m thinking too much on it, but when that feeling comes over me, it feels clear to me — like I’ve been picked out for that, in a way.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: Do you feel that your ability to understand these messages makes you special or different from other people in a way that most others don't realize?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor… I do feel that sometimes. Not all the time in a loud way, but when those things start lining up and I feel like I’m understanding what’s being said, it makes me feel like maybe I see something other people don’t. Like there’s something in me that can pick up on it.\n\nAnd it’s not just about hearing the message. It’s like… once I understand it, I get this feeling that I’m supposed to act on it in some good way. I start thinking, maybe I’m the one who can bring the neighborhood back together again. You know, people used to sit outside, talk, help each other, listen to the older folks. Now everybody is scattered. But when I’m feeling sure of myself, I think I could get them to listen. I could explain it the right way. I could lay out a plan and people would follow it.\n\nI know that might sound like I’m making myself too important, and my wife would probably say, “Now slow down,” but inside it feels real strong. Like I’ve got a special gift with people, like I can make them pay attention when others can’t. So yes, I guess it does make me feel different from other people, like there’s something I’m able to understand and do that most folks don’t see."
  }
]
